## Halong Bay: All Your Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_halong-bay/
Pricing seen on page: ₹40,052 – ₹81,890 per person
Where The Dragons Descend Into The Emerald Waters
The seascape of Halong Bay is one of the most iconic images of Vietnam that you can experience on your trip to Vietnam. Indian tourists can take a cruise through the emerald green waters and explore the numerous rugged islands and islets, including magnificent caves filled with ancient formations. Although Halong Bay is a popular bucket list destination, it is still possible to discover quiet, secluded spots away from the crowds.
Admire Sunset in Cruise in Halong Bay
Halong City is located in the northern part of Vietnam, so it experiences four distinct seasons. The months of April to June are warm and dry, while June to August can be hot and rainy due to the possibility of typhoons. September to December brings cooler weather, eventually becoming cold. Each season has its own unique beauty, with summer being a great time for swimming and sunbathing, while spring and autumn are ideal for enjoying a quieter Halong and engaging in outdoor activities and exploring amusement parks. Peak season is from June to August when the city is crowded, especially on weekends.

### How to get to Halong Bay from India?
You can take a flight from India to Hanoi, the capital of Vietnam. From Hanoi, you can move around in many ways. Besides private cars, there are Limousine cars that provide door-to-door services upon request (Phuc Xuyen, Van Don Xanh, Ha Long Travel…) with ticket prices ranging from 180,000 to 220,000 VND per person. In addition, you can catch a coach at bus stations within Hanoi city, with ticket prices of around 150,000 – 200,000 VND per person.
Quang Ninh has Van Don airport. Therefore, travelers from Ho Chi Minh City and Da Nang can take a direct flight to Quang Ninh, then travel by road from the airport to Ha Long city, which is about 50 km away. There is a bus from the airport to the city center.
In Halong, if you don’t have your own vehicle, you can rent a motorbike for around 120,000 – 200,000 VND per day. If you travel with a group of people like family, friends, or colleagues, it is recommended to rent a taxi (Mai Linh, Thanh Hung, Thinh Hung…), use Grab (bike or car), or rent a private car.

### Attractions in Halong Bay

### Where To Stay in Halong Bay
There are many accommodation options to choose from in Ha Long, including budget hotels, resorts, homestays, guesthouses, and even cruises, ranging in price from a few hundred thousand dong to a few million dong.
Ha Long City is divided into two areas, Bai Chay and Hon Gai, by the Bai Chay bridge. Bai Chay is the tourist hub, with many hotels and restaurants, while Hon Gai is primarily a residential area.
For those looking for a comfortable homestay at an affordable price, options include The Bay, Halong Ginger, Orestay, and Deja Vu House. For more luxurious options, visitors can book a room at hotels such as FLC Grand Hotel on Van Nghe Hill, Wyndham Legend Halong, Novotel, Saigon Halong, Mường Thanh Hotel Group, Royal Lotus, or 5-star resorts such as Vinpearl Resort and Spa and Premier Village Halong Bay Resort.
Spending the night on a cruise ship is one of the most unique and worthwhile experiences when visiting Ha Long Bay. Gone are the crowds and noise of the day; the bay at night is calm and totally different: the water is calm, the reflection of the lights off the cruise ship mirrors the mountains of limestone, the air is cool, and it’s totally relaxing. Waking up in the midst of the morning and witnessing the sun rise over the bay is an experience you will never forget.
A standard night cruise itinerary typically includes:
- Visiting caves, fishing villages, or small islands
- Kayaking or swimming (depending on the schedule)
- Enjoying a romantic dinner on board
- Participating in other evening activities, such as squid fishing, nighttime bay sightseeing, or relaxing on the deck
- Watching the sunrise and having breakfast on the bay
This section is suitable for travelers who wish to experience a one-night stay in the bay, offering a reasonable cost, basic services, a limited itinerary, and a focus on major attractions.
Some popular cruise brands in this category: Phoenix Cruise, Oriental Sails, and Golden Bay Cruise.
This category strikes a balance in terms of cost and quality of services. Compared to 3-star cruises, this category has more beautiful designs, larger rooms, and better services. It is suitable for families, couples, and groups of friends who wish to experience a more comfortable Halong Bay trip compared to regular tours.
Some popular cruise brands in this category: La Pandora Cruise, Athena Cruise, Peony Cruise.
Unlike the above two groups, this category represents a high-end luxury cruise, offering a range of luxurious experiences, privacy, and resort services in a cruise format. Ambassador Cruise is one of the most popular cruise brands, offering large and modern cruise designs. The cruise is equipped with modern facilities such as swimming pools, sky bars, and more, making it a great option for travelers who wish to enjoy a relaxing experience in Halong Bay.
The most popular cruise brand in this category: Ambassador Cruise

### Top things to do in Halong Bay
There’s nothing quite like watching the sun set over the calm waters of Halong Bay, and waking up to a serene morning surrounded by karsts. Relaxing on a sun deck surrounded by magnificent panoramas is the highlight of many a trip.
Explore a floating village
Ha Long Bay is not just a UNESCO-listed site, it’s also home to a number of fishing communities who have lived on the water for centuries. Take a boat tour around a floating village for a glimpse of this rare and beautiful way of life.
Even if you’re not an experienced kayaker, you can’t miss the chance to navigate your way around Ha Long’s gorgeous seascape. Just before sunset is an ideal time to kayak to see quiet lagoons and fishing boats up close.
Beneath their rocks and jungled exteriors, many of Ha Long’s ancient karsts have been carved out by rain and water currents. Take a look inside these geological wonders on foot – some are a squeeze while others are enormous.
Try your hand at rock climbing
There are countless routes in the bay to keep climbing junkies entertained. Deep water soloing is becoming increasingly popular in beautiful Ha Long Bay and Lan Ha Bay, especially on Butterfly Island..
Dining in a natural limestone cave is considered one of the most unique and memorable experiences when visiting Ha Long Bay. Instead of dining on a boat or at a beachfront restaurant, guests are taken by the Ambassador Cruise to a restaurant inside the Dragon Pearl Cave, creating a feeling of complete isolation from the outside world.
Amidst the majestic natural scenery, diners are invited to enjoy a lavish European culinary feast with meticulously prepared dishes, including a high-class vegetarian menu for those who wish. Beyond the cuisine, the highlight of the evening is the show “The Quest for the Sacred Pearl,” inspired by the legend of the Bay. Cuisine, lighting, ambiance, and natural beauty all blend together to create a luxurious and unforgettable experience for your trip to this World Heritage site in Vietnam.
Famous dishes in Halong are mostly made from seafood such as razor clam, mantis shrimp, sandworm, ark shell, shrimp, crab, fish, squid… Seafood noodle soup is very popular.
- Razor clam noodle soup: a common breakfast and lunch dish in Halong. Razor clam (also known as mantis shrimp) is a seafood species abundant in Halong Bay, with firm, sweet and nutritious flesh. A bowl of noodle soup costs around 30,000 – 40,000 VND.
- Stuffed squid cakes: a very famous specialty of Halong, can be enjoyed at any time of the day in Halong. Some famous addresses include the brands of Ms. Ngan, Ms. Yen, Goc Bang restaurant, or at the local market during the day. A serving costs between 30,000 – 55,000 VND. The famous sandworm address in Halong is Mrs. Ty’s Sam at No. 6 Cao Thang Alley, or Sam Quang Yen restaurant, located on the road from Halong to Hanoi. In the afternoon at the snack area of Halong market 1, there is also a stall selling sour-sweet sandworm feet, sandworm cakes… which are quite delicious.
- Stir-fried snails: a popular snack that locals love, with affordable prices ranging around 35,000 VND/dish depending on the type. To enjoy stir-fried snails, you should go to street stalls where you can feel more comfortable and the cooking style is more rustic. Stir-fried snails with soy sauce, lemongrass, tamarind sauce, coconut… are very tasty and addictive.
- Tran chau yogurt: a must-try dessert, having yogurt served with pearls and been popular for about 10 years. This famous snack has become a brand of Halong and is well-known in other cities. The stalls make their own smooth, non-icy yogurt that is cool and refreshing. A serving costs only 10,000 – 30,000 VND. Mrs. Nghi’s shop or Tuan Lien’s shop are familiar places for tourists as they have spacious parking lots. Tourists can also try Mrs. Phuong’s yogurt, which is highly recommended by many locals.

## Hanoi Travel: All You Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_hanoi/
Pricing seen on page: ₹40,052 – ₹81,890 per person
Where Timeless Heritage and Vibrant Spirit Intersect
Hanoi, the capital of Vietnam definitely deserves a top spot on your list of must-visit destinations when planning your Vietnam trip. This vibrant city is considered the heart and soul of Vietnam and was founded more than a millennium ago and boasts a rich history. The Old Quarter, with its 14th-century streets, is a testament to its past.
Strolling through the area’s tree-lined lanes and colonial facades will take you back in time. At the same time, the modern cafes, world-class restaurants, and art galleries are giving the ancient city a new modern life. When the sun sets, Hanoi’s watering holes offer a variety of options, from sophisticated rooftop bars to bustling “bia hoi” spots.
Hanoi’s weather is most pleasant between April and June. May is particularly beautiful because the trees suddenly burst into bloom. September and October are also excellent months to visit because the temperatures are cooler, and the sky is clearer.

### How to get to Hanoi from India
The most convenient way to get to Hanoi from India is by air. There are several airlines that operate flights from major Indian cities like Delhi, Mumbai, and Kolkata to Hanoi’s Noi Bai International Airport. Some of the airlines that offer this route include Vietnam Airlines, Vietjet Air, and Indigo…
There are many options for getting around Hanoi, including taxis, buses, and motorbikes. Grab and other ride-hailing apps offer on-demand services for getting around. Hanoi’s bus network has an extensive network around the city with low-cost fares. Lastly, you can hire a classic cyclo for a sightseeing tour of the Old Quarter.
Hanoi offers a variety of accommodation options for Indian tourists, including budget guesthouses, homestays, hotels, and resorts that cater to different budgets and preferences. If you’re exploring the Old Quarter, you can rent a “backpacker-style” dorm room bed for around 200,000 VND per night, or splurge on a high-end hotel for 3,000,000 – 5,000,000 VND per night.
Some notable hotels include the Sofitel Legend Metropole Hanoi, which boasts French colonial architecture and is located just a few steps from the Hanoi Opera House. The Hilton Hanoi Opera, Apricot Hotel on the shore of Hoan Kiem Lake, Sheraton Hanoi with its renowned West Lake view, and InterContinental Hanoi Westlake are also worth considering. The Lotte Hotel Hanoi, situated on the top floors of the 65-storey Lotte Center, is another upscale option.

### Top things to do in Hanoi
- Sample the street food: To get a taste of Hanoi’s authenticity, sample the street food. The street kitchens of the Old Quarter offer a range of delicacies. Phở broth, infused with star anise, steams on every corner, while the scent of bún chả fills the air as barbecued pork sizzles over hot coals.
- Stroll the Old Quarter: Hanoi is a sensory overload. Ancient temples emit wisps of incense into the streets, and the clang of blacksmiths’ hammers mixes with the calls of mobile fruit sellers. Hop on a cyclo and tour this fascinating maze.
- Explore Hanoi’s cafe culture: Hanoi’s cafe culture is a must-experience. Though fast-paced on the surface, the true rhythm of Hanoi life is far from hurried and is reflected well in its leisurely coffee hours. Alongside traditional coffee houses, a growing number of unique cafes serve new brews in cool caffeine dens.
- Check out the art scene: The elegant Fine Arts Museum houses the country’s most important collection, including ancient Cham artifacts and impressionist pieces. For something more contemporary, head to Manzi or the Vietnam Art Gallery.
- Join the locals at Hoan Kiem Lake: Hanoi is Vietnam’s art capital. The elegant Fine Arts Museum houses the country’s most important collection, including ancient Cham artifacts and impressionist pieces. For something more contemporary, head to Manzi or the Vietnam Art Gallery.
In brief, the Old Quarter of Hanoi is a sensory overload. Ancient temples emit wisps of incense into the streets, and the clang of blacksmiths’ hammers mixes with the calls of mobile fruit sellers. Hop on a cyclo and tour this fascinating maze.
Food in Hanoi showcases a perfect blend of sour, spicy, salty, sweet flavors, reflecting the city’s vibrant, contemplative, captivating and appealing image. You can easily find Hanoi’s specialties at street food stalls and restaurant in every corners of Hanoi:
- Phở gà (Chicken rice noodle soup): can be perfect for Indian eaters who don’t consume beef or pork. Some recommended places are Quan Nguyet on Phu Doan Street, Cham on Yen Ninh Street, Lan Ong’s mixed chicken pho or rolled pho in Ngu Xa.
- Bún (vermicelli): Hanoi has many types of bun dishes, including bun cha, bun thang, bun oc, bun moc, and bun doc mung. Among them, bun thang, a colorful, elegant and mouth-watering noodle soup with chicken, egg, and herbs, is a must-try. For bun oc (snail vermicelli soup), you can try it at restaurants on Hai Ba Trung, Dang Dung, and Hoe Nhai streets.
- Chả cá Lã Vọng (La Vong grilled fish): made from the finest fish, especially snakehead fish, and is cooked with a variety of herbs and spices, often served with grilled rice paper, noodles, and shrimp paste. Popular restaurants that serve chả cá Lã Vọng include Chả cá Lã Vọng on 14 Cha Ca street, Chả cá Thăng Long on 21 Duong Thanh street, and Chả cá Lão Ngư on 171 Thai Ha street.
- Coffee: is a characteristic feature of Hanoi with many long-standing cafes in the Old Quarter. Egg coffee is a specialty of Hanoi made by whipping egg into Vietnamese coffee, served in a small cup with a spoon to enjoy the frothy layer on top before sipping the coffee underneath. Famous egg coffee shops include Quán Đinh, Giảng, Cà phê Phố Cổ, Loading T, and All Day Coffee, with a price ranging from 25,000 – 50,000 VND per cup.

### Gift ideas for visitors to Hanoi
- O mai (dried fruits): a popular snack in Hanoi, with a unique blend of sour, spicy, salty, and sweet flavors. Hang Duong Street is the go-to place to buy this treat, with options including apricot, plum, ginger, tamarind, peach, lime, kumquat, rose, peach, frog, and tamarind. Tamarind O mai is recommended for those in southern Vietnam. Prices range from 100,000 – 150,000 VND per kilogram.
- Banh com Hang Than: a great substitute for Vong village’s cốm, a young green rice with a unique aroma that is only available in the fall. Bánh cốm has a soft outer layer made from sticky rice flour and a mung bean and coconut filling with a natural pomelo scent.
- Tra sen (West Lake lotus tea): a precious gift made through a meticulous process of soaking dried tea leaves with lotus grains to absorb the fragrance and flavor. The lotus used for tea must be grown in ponds around West Lake. Although it is only available in May and June, the lotus fragrance is preserved year-round and enjoyed by tourists.
- Van Phuc silk: thin, soft, breathable, and durable, making it a popular choice for scarves, clothes, dresses, bags, and more. You can find silk products in Van Phuc village or at stands along Hang Gai or Dinh Liet Street.
- Bat Trang pottery: known for its enamel colors and exquisite handcrafted techniques. You can find pottery bowls, plates, and other products in this village.

## Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam: Ultimate Guide For Indian Tourists
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_ho-chi-minh-city/
Pricing seen on page: ₹55,275 – ₹96,705 per person

### Ho Chi Minh City (Saigon), Vietnam
The Pearl of the Far East – The City That Never Sleeps
Ho Chi Minh City, formerly known as Saigon, is a bustling metropolis in Southern Vietnam that offers a unique blend of modernity and tradition. It is a city that is alive with energy, where you can experience a rich cultural heritage, taste delicious local cuisine, and shop for everything from luxury goods to handmade souvenirs.
If you’re planning a Vietnam trip from India, Ho Chi Minh City could be your ideal destination. The city is home to a vibrant Indian community and offers a variety of options for vegetarian and halal food. With its warm climate, affordable prices, and friendly locals, Ho Chi Minh City is sure to leave a lasting impression on any traveler.
Ho Chi Minh City at Night
Ho Chi Minh City experiences warm temperatures throughout the year, with a hot period from April to May and a rainy season from May to November. Temperatures are slightly cooler in December and January.

### How to get to Ho Chi Minh City from India
If you’re traveling to Ho Chi Minh City (also known as Saigon) from India, the best option is to take a flight. There are a certain number of direct flights from India to Ho Chi Minh City provided by Vietnam Airlines, Vietjet Air and Indigo, departing from different cities in India.
Once you arrive at Tan Son Nhat International Airport in Ho Chi Minh City, you can take a taxi, ride-hailing app, or bus to your hotel or destination. Taxis and ride-hailing apps are readily available at the airport, and fares are reasonable. The airport is also well-connected by public buses that serve various parts of the city, with fares ranging from 5,000-40,000 VND depending on the distance.

### Getting around Ho Chi Minh City
There are several ways to get around Ho Chi Minh City:
- Taxi: Taxis are widely available in Saigon and are a convenient way to get around. Be sure to choose reputable taxi companies such as Mai Linh or Vinasun to avoid scams.
- Grab/ride-hailing apps: Grab is a popular ride-hailing app in Vietnam, allowing users to book rides from their smartphones. It’s a convenient and safe option for getting around Saigon.
- Motorbike rental: You can rent motorbikes from many shops in Saigon, with prices ranging from around 100,000 VND to 200,000 VND per day. However, driving in Saigon can be chaotic and challenging for inexperienced riders.
- Public transportation: Saigon has an extensive bus network, with fares starting at around 5,000 VND per ride. However, the buses can be crowded and challenging to navigate for non-Vietnamese speakers.
- Walking: For those who prefer to explore on foot, Saigon has several pedestrian-friendly areas such as Ben Thanh Market and Nguyen Hue Walking Street. However, be mindful of the traffic and the hot, humid climate.

### Top Lists in Ho Chi Minh City

### Attractions in Ho Chi Minh City
The best district to stay in Ho Chi Minh City for short-term visitors (in my opinion) is District 1. I would recommend staying near Ben Thanh Market or Bui Vien Walking Street as it allows easy access to the best attractions in the city. However, District 3 and District 5 are also good options as they are close to the city center and offer plenty of activities. District 2 (Thao Dien) is another option with a great art and expat scene, but it is further away from the city center.
For those who want to indulge in luxury, the city has plenty of high-end hotels that offer top-notch amenities and services. Some of the most popular options include the Park Hyatt Saigon, InterContinental Saigon, and Caravelle Saigon.
For travelers on a budget, there are many affordable hostels and guesthouses available in the city. Some of the best options include The Common Room Project, Saigon Backpackers Hostel, and CCentral Hotel.
If you’re looking for a more local experience, consider staying in a homestay or Airbnb. This is a great way to immerse yourself in the local culture and get a taste of daily life in the city.

### Top things to do in Ho Chi Minh City
- See the top landmarks: visit the Notre Dame Cathedral, the city’s Central Post Office, Saigon Opera House, Ben Thanh Market and the Jade Emperor Pagoda.
- Shop small boutiques: explore old buildings such as 22 Ly Tu Trong and 42 Nguyen Hue that have been repurposed as shopping centers with all kinds of fascinating stores.
- Explore the Chinese Quarter: walk around Cho Lon and visit Binh Tay Market and Thien Hau Pagoda.
- Hop on a motorcycle: take a motorbike tour in the evening to see the city at its most romantic and its sidewalk vendors.
- Enjoying Saigon street food: try local favorites such as Bánh Mì, Bánh Xèo, and Cơm Tấm at roadside eateries for incredible roadside feasts
Saigon offers an endless variety of exquisite and reasonably priced selections, making it a street food lover’s delight. Bánh Mì (a Vietnamese sandwich), Phở (noodle soup), Bánh Xèo (a savory pancake), Cơm Tấm (broken rice), and Bún Thịt Nướng are some dishes you must try (grilled pork with vermicelli noodles).
There are additional options like Trứng Vịt Lộn (balut), Ốc (snails), and Bột Chiên for intrepid eaters (fried rice cake).
Ben Thanh Market, Binh Tay Market, and the street food alley on Nguyen Thuong Hien Street are a few of the greatest locations in Saigon to sample street cuisine. The Saigon street food scene is not to be missed, so make sure to bring your hunger and an open mind!

### FAQs about Ho Chi Minh City
Yes, Indian citizens need a visa to enter Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam. They can apply for a visa online or through the Vietnam Embassy in India.
The best way to get around Ho Chi Minh City is by taxi, motorbike taxi, or using ride-hailing apps like Grab. The city also has a reliable public transportation system, including buses and metro lines.
It’s important to note that traffic in Ho Chi Minh City can be very congested, so it’s best to plan accordingly when choosing transportation options.
The best time to visit Ho Chi Minh City from India is between December and April when the weather is dry and pleasant.
Some must-visit attractions in Ho Chi Minh City for Indian travelers include the War Remnants Museum, Reunification Palace, Notre Dame Cathedral, Ben Thanh Market, and the Cu Chi Tunnels.
Ho Chi Minh City has a wide range of vegan-friendly restaurants that cater to plant-based diets. Some of the popular options include:

## Hoi An: All Your Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_hoi-an/
Pricing seen on page: ₹40,052 – ₹81,890 per person
A Little Old Town Where History And Culture Come Alive.
Hoi An is a destination where life slows down to a more relaxed pace, making it a perfect stop during your trip to Vietnam.
This heritage city was once a bustling trading port, and today, visitors can see the Thu Bon River flowing past old shop houses and ancient pagodas, with sampans docking at the ferry quay. Beyond the Old Town, narrow roads cut through verdant rice fields and lead to a stunning coastline. Spending a few days here, you may find yourself enchanted by Hoi An’s charming beauty, especially at night when the town is lit up with lanterns.
The climate in Hoi An blends the weather patterns of the South and the North, with distinct rainy and dry seasons. Each season in the old town has its own unique beauty.
First three months of the year: Best time to visit Hoi An when the sun is not too intense, cool weather with occasional light rain.
From April to June: The peak of the dry season (also the peak tourist season), but the weather is still pleasant due to its location in the southern Truong Son range.
During the rainy season: Many tourists still come to Hoian for unique experiences. July is still part of the dry season. August and September mark the transition period. The weather is still slightly sunny, with occasional light rain and coolness at night.

### How to get to Hoi An from India
The most common way to get to Hoi An from India is to take a flight to Da Nang International Airport, which is located about 30 kilometers away from Hoi An. Several airlines, including Air India, Vietnam Airlines, and VietJet Air, operate direct flights between major Indian cities like Delhi and Mumbai and Da Nang.
From Da Nang Airport, visitors can take a taxi, private car or shuttle bus to Hoi An. The journey takes about 45 minutes to an hour, depending on traffic conditions. Another option is to take a train to Da Nang and then continue the journey to Hoi An by taxi or shuttle bus. The train journey takes longer but offers scenic views of the Vietnamese countryside.
Arriving at Da Nang airport, visitors can choose to take a shuttle bus or a taxi, private car, or even hire their own car to travel to the center of Hoi An. For those who prefer to have more flexibility in their transportation, they can rent a motorbike from Da Nang city to enjoy Hoi An, with prices around 150,000 VND per day. To get around Hoi An, visitors can use bicycles or rented motorbikes from the hotels or homestays they are staying in. Bicycle rentals cost around 30,000 VND per day, while motorbike rentals cost around 120,000 VND per day.
In Hoi An, summer is the most popular season for tourists, so it’s best to book accommodation early. There are many new resorts in beautiful locations like Cam Chau, An Bang Beach, and Cua Dai. Homestays have also become popular and offer unique architecture, beautiful gardens, and affordable prices, starting at around 200,000 VND per night. Some popular homestays include Maison de Tau, Under The Coconut Tree, An Bang Beach Hideaway, Heron House, and Le Bleu.

### Top things to do in Hoi An
- Get lost in the Old Town: Explore the unique architectural fusion of Hoi An’s Old Town, with influences from China, Japan, and Europe. Visit iconic landmarks like the Japanese Bridge, Tan Ky ancestral house, and Phuc Kien Assembly Hall.
- Treat your taste buds: Experience the delightful cuisine of Hoi An, with fresh ingredients sourced from local farms and fishing boats. Savor the flavors of Cao Lau, Hoanh Thanh, and white rose dumplings.
- Cycle in the countryside: Rent a bike and explore the countryside during the peaceful hours of sunrise. Enjoy the scenic views of green paddy fields, frond-fringed ponds, and water buffaloes in Cam Thanh and Tra Que.
- Have a beach day: Relax on the sandy stretches of An Bang Beach, which is just a 10-minute drive from the Ancient Town. Enjoy fresh seafood lunches and the cool waves, or venture out to the Cham Islands for pristine shores.
- Marvel at My Son Sanctuary: Visit the My Son Sanctuary, a UNESCO Cultural Heritage Site located 40 kilometers from Hoi An. These temples, built by the Cham people, date back to the 7th to 13th century, and are nestled in a valley near a shaded stream.
- Have tailor-made clothing: Hoi An is definitely the go-to place when it comes to custom tailoring. Tailored clothes in Hoi An are just a fraction of the price. The prices are not only cheaper but the products are also of good quality. There is a good balance of high-end tailor shops and there are other tailor shops that are cheap and can perfectly make basic clothes.
When visiting Hoi An, you never have to worry about going hungry or lacking food choices. From early morning until late at night, the streets of Hoi An are always bustling with food vendors and restaurants. Prices are also very affordable, with street-side desserts like sweet soup ranging from 5,000 to 10,000 VND per cup, banh mi (Vietnamese sandwich) priced between 15,000 to 25,000 VND per loaf, and signature dishes like cao lau and mi Quang only costing 25,000 VND.
Some of the most famous dining spots that both local and foreign tourists cannot miss include Ba Buoi’s chicken rice, Phuong’s banh mi, Madam Khanh’s banh mi, Gieng Ba Le’s banh xeo, vegetarian food at Hoi An market, Mot Hoi An herbal drink, and Thanh’s cao lau.
Hoi An’s street food scene is also renowned for its sweet treats, including ice cream rolls, various sweet soups, lotus seed sweet soup, mung bean sweet soup, tofu pudding, mango cakes, and pigskin cakes…
- Lanterns are a popular gift in Hoi An. You should look for large shops that both make and sell lanterns, to have a diverse selection of designs, sizes, and materials.
- Bronze statues are sold all over the old town, priced from 130,000 to 350,000 VND depending on the size. These are all products of the famous Phuoc Kieu bronze casting village in Hoi An.
- Clay figurines are a souvenir product from Thanh Ha pottery village in Hoi An. While not diverse in design, they are still popular due to their low price, around 5,000 VND.
- Hoi An’s woodworking village, Kim Bong, is famous for its carved wooden items. Artisans all come from this village, and some have opened their own workshops in the old town. The price of a wooden item ranges from 120,000 to 300,000 VND.
- Hoi An silk is known for its cool, light, and soft texture. In addition to clothing, bags, and scarves that are readily available, you can also have a tailor make a custom design for you and receive it within a day. The average price for a silk scarf is 150,000 VND.
If you want to buy food as a gift from Hoi An, suggested options are green bean cakes, banh thuan cakes, phu the cakes, and chili sauce.

The most common way to travel from India to Hoi An, Vietnam is by air. There are direct flights available from major Indian cities like Delhi, Mumbai, and Bangalore to Da Nang, which is the closest airport to Hoi An. From Da Nang, you can take a taxi or bus to Hoi An.
You may want to avoid visiting Hoi An during the rainy season from September to January when there can be flooding and heavy rains. The peak tourist season from June to August can also be crowded and expensive. Consider visiting during the shoulder season (February to May) or the low season (September to January) for lower prices and fewer tourists.
Hoi An has a tropical climate, so it’s best to pack light, breathable clothing made of natural fibers. Don’t forget to pack comfortable shoes for walking, a hat or sunscreen for protection from the sun, and insect repellent.
Hoi An is known for its well-preserved Ancient Town, which is a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Other popular attractions include the Japanese Covered Bridge, the Hoi An Night Market, and the nearby Marble Mountains.
Yes, having clothes tailored in Hoi An is a popular activity for tourists, and the town has many skilled tailors who can make clothes for people of all sizes and shapes, including Indians. In fact, some tailors in Hoi An specialize in making traditional Indian clothing, such as saris and kurta pajamas, and can help you find the right fabrics and designs to suit your preferences. Just be sure to allow enough time for the tailoring process, as it typically takes a few days to a week to complete a garment.

## Visit Hue: All Your Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_hue/
Pricing seen on page: ₹42,335 – ₹53,415 per person
The Imperial City Of Heritage, The Land Of Poetry
Hue, a city nestled in Vietnam’s central region, boasts a rich cultural and historical heritage that is sure to hold anyone’s heart, especially for Indian travelers on their trip to Vietnam. The Nguyen Dynasty Kings established their feudal capital on the verdant riverbanks and rolling hills of Hue, and their imperial legacy still echoes through the city’s streets, monuments, and architecture. But that’s not all – Hue’s cuisine is a gastronomic delight, with its aromatic and flavorful dishes sure to tantalize your taste buds.
As you wander through the city, you’ll be charmed by the mossy pagodas, art deco mansions, and vibrant markets, all nestled amidst lush foliage. And then there’s the Huong River, which flows serenely through the heart of Hue, lending a tranquil and soothing vibe to the city. Hue is truly a feast for the senses, and a must-visit destination for ones seeking to immerse themselves in Vietnam’s cultural heritage.
The tourist season in Hue is from November of the previous year to April of the following year, with the best time to visit being the first three to four months of the year when the weather is cool. For those who love mountains, beaches, sunrises, and sunsets, the best time to visit is from June to August when the sun is at its best and the sea is the bluest.
Hue doesn’t have a dry season, only a rainy season and a less rainy season. The less rainy season usually lasts from March to August, with hot and oppressive weather. From September to January of the following year is the rainy season, with cold weather. Especially from October to November is the stormy season with heavy and persistent rains.
Spring lasts from the end of January to March, with beautiful weather, light sunshine, and occasionally chilly weather. From April to July, summer is hot in Hue, and it is an ideal tourist destination for beach lovers. During summer, the ancient city is decorated with the yellow of orchids, the pink of peach blossoms, and the purple of Bang Lang flowers. August marks the beginning of autumn, the most beautiful season of the year in Hue.

### How to get to Hue from India
To reach Hue from India, the most convenient option is to take a flight to Da Nang International Airport, which is the closest airport to Hue. There are direct flights available from major Indian cities such as Delhi and Mumbai to Da Nang via connecting flights. From the airport, you can take a taxi or a shuttle bus to Hue, which is about 100 kilometers away.
Alternatively, you can take a train or a bus from major cities in Vietnam, such as Hanoi or Ho Chi Minh City, to Hue. The journey by train or bus can take anywhere from 12 to 18 hours, depending on the mode of transport and the route taken. Once you reach Hue, you can hire a motorbike or take a taxi to explore the city and its surroundings.
In Hue, to save costs and have the freedom to stop and explore along the way, it is recommended to rent a motorbike from your place of accommodation. The rental price for a standard motorbike is around 80,000 VND per day, while an automatic scooter costs around 130,000 VND per day, not including fuel.
One fun way to explore Hue that visitors should try is by taking a cyclo ride. The cyclo drivers also act as tour guides, taking visitors to popular destinations in the city such as Truong Tien Bridge and Dong Ba Market. At each destination, the cyclo will stop and allow visitors to explore on their own. Tours usually last for an hour, with prices ranging from 50,000 to 120,000 VND depending on the destination and travel time.
Hotels in Hue are reasonably priced. It is recommended to book accommodation in the city center, close to the train and bus stations for easy transportation and access to local tourist attractions and food spots.
Homestays and hostels located in the city center such as A-Maze House, Sunshine, To Vo, and Tram offer rates of only 100,000 – 200,000 VND per night, which is suitable for solo travelers or groups of young friends. If traveling in a group, it is recommended to book in advance through online booking channels such as Booking, Agoda, Traveloka, Vntrip for better prices.
Hue also has a variety of luxury 4-5 star hotels and resorts. Famous brands include Banyan Tree Lang Co, Laguna Lang Co, Pilgrimage Village Boutique Resort & Spa, La Poet Hotel, Lapochine Beach Resort, Silk Path Grand Hue Hotel & Spa, Indochine Palace, Imperial Hotel Hue, Azerai La Residence Hue, and Vinpearl. Room rates range from approximately 2,000,000 – 8,000,000 VND per night.
- See Imperial Vietnam: Explore the palaces, pavilions, and theaters of the Hue Citadel and visit the tombs of emperors Tu Duc, Minh Mang and Khai Dinh.
- Eat like a king: Sample Hue’s world-class cuisine by visiting Dong Ba Market and trying their much-loved snacks like Banh Khoai and Banh Beo.
- Explore the city’s Buddhist roots: Visit Thien Mu Pagoda with its iconic seven-tiered tower and Tu Hieu Monastery, where Thich Nhat Hanh spent his days as a novice monk.
- Cycle in the rice paddies: Experience Hue’s beautiful countryside on a bike ride to the tile-roofed Thanh Toan Bridge, passing vegetable farms, footbridges, and flocks of ducks.
- Revisit colonial history: Take a stroll down Le Loi and see the remaining French influence in Hue’s architecture, including the art deco lines of La Residence Hotel and the stately crimson buildings of Quoc Hoc High School.
Hue cuisine has long been famous for its delicious and aesthetically pleasing dishes. Some must-try dishes include bun bo, com hen, banh canh, banh beo, nam, loc, banh ep, banh uot thit nuong, and che. Each dish costs between 7,000 – 20,000 VND, while more filling options like bun bo and com niu cost around 30,000 VND. With just 100,000 – 300,000 VND, you can have a satisfying meal.
For drinks, try Tao Pho, soy milk, che, and banh loc with roasted pork. These drinks start at 15,000 VND and are available at many street vendors. Che Mo Ton Dich near Thuong Bac Park is a popular spot for Hue-style che.
To find authentic Huế cuisine, try looking for places located in small alleys or with old, simple signs. Places with names that start with “Mụ,” “Mệ,” “O,” or “Bé” are usually run by Huế natives and offer the most authentic dishes. Additionally, the older the owner, the better the food.
Here are some suggested food addresses in Hue:
- Bun bo Hue at 47 Nguyen Cong Tru, 20 Bach Đang, 17 Hung Vuong…
- Banh beo nam loc at O Le, alley 104/17/9 Kim Long
- Banh Hue at Luu Ngoc, 70 Kim Long
- Sua chua (yogurt) is available at many locations throughout the city.
- Grilled meat wrapped in rice paper at Huyen Anh, 52/11 Kim Long
- Com hen at Hoa Đong, 64 alley 7 Ung Binh
- Boiled meat rolled in sour shrimp sauce at the An Cuu riverbank
- Che Ong Lac, 36 Thanh Tinh
- Che Hue Kinh Do, 65 Tran Hung Dao
- Pho Tay Hue (walking street)
If you want to buy souvenirs, consider Hue royal tea, lotus tea, nem cha (grilled pork roll), various kinds of fish sauce, dau tram (essential oil), dried banh ép, sesame candy, conical hats, or souvenir items with images of Hue’s landscapes.

Vietnam Love Story: A Soulful Journey from HCMC to Hanoi
The most convenient way to travel from India to Hue is by air. You can take a flight from major Indian cities like Delhi, Mumbai, Bangalore, or Kolkata to Da Nang International Airport, which is the nearest airport to Hue. From there, you can hire a taxi or take a bus to reach Hue.
Hue offers a wealth of attractions that are perfect for Indian travelers. The Imperial Citadel, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, is a must-visit destination, where visitors can admire the majestic palaces, pavilions, and gardens of the Nguyen Dynasty. Another must-visit site is the Thien Mu Pagoda, a seven-story pagoda that dates back to the 17th century, located on a hill overlooking the Perfume River. The Tomb of Emperor Khai Dinh, with its intricate mosaics and European-influenced architecture, is another popular attraction.
For those interested in learning more about Hue’s history and culture, a visit to the Hue Museum of Royal Antiquities is recommended, where visitors can see a collection of royal artifacts and learn about the city’s imperial past. The Dong Ba Market is also worth a visit, where visitors can experience the bustling local market and try some of Hue’s famous street food.
Lastly, taking a boat ride along the Perfume River, enjoying the scenic views of the surrounding hills and pagodas, is a must-do activity while in Hue.

## Mekong Delta: All Your Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_mekong-delta/
Pricing seen on page: ₹42,335 – ₹81,890 per person
Life In The Mekong Delta Flows As Smoothly As The River That Feeds It
Nestled in the southernmost part of Vietnam lies a region of unparalleled beauty – The Mekong Delta. Often called the “Southwestern region” or “Western region” by locals, this charming land of water and greenery is also known as the Nine Dragon River Delta.
With 12 provinces and 1 centrally-governed city, the Mekong Delta can offer you a chance to experience the warm hospitality and down-to-earth nature of the Vietnamese people. Those who visited this magical region often say they were embarking on a journey of a lifetime. So, why not consider adding this special destination to your list of your Vietnam tour package?

### The Mekong Delta weather & geographical characteristics
Due to its tropical climate near the equator, the Mekong Delta has an average temperature of around 28°C which is stable throughout the year. The region experiences two distinct seasons: the rainy season (from May to November) and the dry season (from December to April). The flooding season typically lasts from around July to November in the lunar calendar, with some areas flooding from September to October depending on the year.
Because of the influence of the coastal and riverine environment, over time the Mekong Delta region has formed and developed a diverse range of natural ecosystems. These include freshwater wetland ecosystems (such as Tam Nong National Park, Tra Su forest, and Dong Thap Muoi region), coastal mangrove ecosystems (such as U Minh Thuong and U Minh Ha National Parks), and agricultural ecosystems.

### How to get to Mekong Delta from India
To get to the Mekong Delta from India, you will need to fly to Ho Chi Minh City, which is the closest international airport. From there, you can take a bus or hire a private car to go to the Mekong Delta, which is approximately 170 kilometers away. There are also organized tours available from Ho Chi Minh City that include transportation to the Mekong Delta and a guided tour of the region. Be sure to check visa requirements for travel to Vietnam as well.

### Getting around Mekong Delta
You can explore the Mekong Delta region in Vietnam by renting a car or motorbike, taking a bus, hiring a private boat or joining a tour, cycling or taking a taxi. However, be aware that roads may not be well-maintained and traffic can be chaotic. Buses may not be very comfortable and taxis can be more expensive than other options. Keep in mind that the infrastructure may not be as well-developed in some areas of the Mekong Delta compared to more urbanized areas of Vietnam.
Boating is also a popular activity in the Mekong Delta region of Vietnam, as it is crisscrossed by many waterways. Visitors can hire a private boat or join a tour to explore the various sights along the river. Some popular destinations for boating include the floating markets, fruit orchards, and traditional villages. It is a unique way to experience the local culture and way of life in the delta region. However, visitors should be aware of safety precautions when boating, such as wearing life jackets and avoiding overloaded or unstable boats.

### Attractions in Mekong Delta
There are various types of accommodation available in the Mekong Delta region, ranging from budget-friendly options to more upscale choices.
Budget-friendly options include guesthouses, hostels, and homestays where you can stay with local families and experience the local culture. These options usually offer basic amenities such as a bed, shared bathroom facilities, and some meals.
Mid-range options include hotels and resorts with more amenities such as air conditioning, private bathrooms, swimming pools, and restaurants. These options are generally more comfortable and convenient for travelers.
For more upscale options, there are luxury resorts and boutique hotels that offer premium amenities such as spas, fitness centers, and high-end dining options.
It is important to note that accommodation options in some areas of the Mekong Delta may be limited, especially in more remote areas. It is advisable to book in advance, especially during peak travel seasons.

### Best experiences in Mekong Delta

**1. Exploring Mekong Delta’s nature**
When visiting this land, besides taking boat trips through the waterways which have become a trademark, tourists can also immerse themselves in the peaceful natural environment on small boats and explore the countryside, witnessing bustling floating markets on the river and wandering through fruit-laden orchards. Moreover, they can discover the diverse ecosystem of the mangrove forests characteristic of this region.

**2. Visit Ba Duc Ancient House in Tien Giang**
Located in the Mekong Delta, the Ba Duc Ancient House in Hoa Hop, Cai Be, Tien Giang is a standout example of the blending of traditional Vietnamese and French colonial architecture. The 19th and early 20th-century Catholic church towers rise above the riverside villages. This famous villa has a tropical fruit and bonsai garden, attracting many visitors.

**3. Explore Tra Su cajuput forest**
The Tra Su cajuput forest is located in Van Giao commune, An Giang province. This flooded forest is typical of the western Hau River region, home to many species of animals and plants in the forest system in Vietnam. Visitors can reach it by a high-speed boat crossing the more than 1,500-hectare mangrove forest, and nothing is more wonderful than taking a paddle boat tour with local people to explore life of the species here.

**4. Enjoy Water and Moon Festival in Phnom Penh**
The Water and Moon Festival is held annually in Phnom Penh, witnessing hundreds of thousands of Cambodians pouring onto the streets to participate in the festival. This important cultural event lasts for three days and marks the end of the rainy season and the remarkable reversal of the Tonle Sap River’s flow. During the festival, visitors can enjoy the exciting races between traditional Khmer boats.
A visit to the Angkor temple complex is a top choice for any first-time visitor to Siem Reap. If visitors take a Spirit cruise tour, they will be treated to a unique dinner and traditional Angkor cultural performance. Watching the sunrise at Angkor Wat temple and taking a balloon ride to view this historical monument from above are fascinating experiences for tourists.

### Mekong Delta’s unique cuisine and fruit paradise
Food: Cuisine in the Mekong Delta region reflects the culture of a riverine countryside. The main foods in the area are still rice and seafood, but there is a wide variety of dishes available.
People in the Mekong Delta are very meticulous about the quality of their food and are creative in preparing dishes to change up the taste. For instance, with snakehead fish, it can be prepared in many different ways such as braised, sour soup, congee, grilled, dried… The culinary culture also combines and creates many delicious, unique and exotic dishes. Travelers can explore many unique and delicious dishes in the area, such as sour soup, braised fish, fermented hotpot, rice noodle soup, fermented noodle soup, elephant ear fish soup, simmered duck…
Fruits: Known as the fruit basket of the Southern region, the Mekong Delta is the birthplace of many famous and delicious fruits such as soursop, green-skinned pomelo, durian, pink mandarin, rambutan, mangosteen, longan, jackfruit… During their journey, tourists will have the opportunity to enjoy the fragrant and delicious flavors of these fruits.

It is difficult to determine if the Mekong Delta is favored by Indian tourists as it depends on individual preferences and interests. However, the Mekong Delta is a popular destination for tourists from all over the world, including India, due to its unique culture, natural beauty, and diverse cuisine. Many Indian tourists may be interested in exploring the Mekong Delta’s floating markets, rice paddies, fruit orchards, and historic landmarks. Additionally, the region offers a variety of activities such as boat tours, cycling, and homestays, which may appeal to Indian tourists seeking an immersive cultural experience.
The best time to visit the Mekong Delta is from November to April when the weather is dry and cool.
You can take a direct flight from India to Ho Chi Minh City, which is the largest city in Vietnam and the gateway to the Mekong Delta. From Ho Chi Minh City, you can take a bus or taxi to the Mekong Delta.
Some of the must-see attractions in the Mekong Delta include the floating markets, rice paddies, fruit orchards, coconut candy factories, and traditional villages.
The food in the Mekong Delta is known for its fresh and flavorful ingredients, such as fish, shrimp, rice, and tropical fruits. Some popular dishes include banh xeo (Vietnamese crepe), hu tieu (noodle soup), and ca kho to (caramelized fish).

## Nha Trang, Vietnam: Ultimate Guide For Indian Tourists (2026)
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_nha-trang/
Eastern Pearl, Where The Sun, Sand, And Sea Meet To Create Paradise On Earth
Nha Trang is the most beautiful coastal city in the central region of the coastal area. The gem of Khanh Hoa province is always a highlight on Vietnam’s tourism map, attracting many domestic and international tourists.
Nha Trang has clear blue beaches, white sand, as well as offshore islands with stunning beauty, magical coral reefs beneath the ocean, and fresh seafood from the sea. The city of Nha Trang is an ideal destination for those who love both beach tourism and resort vacations.
Nha Trang also has many attractive tourist spots such as pretty islands; Ninh Van Bay; Oceanographic Institute; Ponagar Tower; Dai Lanh Beach. In addition, Nha Trang is rich in local specialties (Ninh Hoa fermented pork rolls, rice crackers, tamarind squid, bird’s nest, etc.).

### Best time to visit Nha Trang
The average temperature in Nha Trang is 26.5ºC, and it is divided into two main seasons: the rainy season and the dry season.
The period from January to September is the dry season in Nha Trang. Particularly, most tourists come to Nha Trang between April and June. At this time, the weather is early summer, so the heat is not too intense. You can fully enjoy the cool and pleasant seawater. Occasionally, Nha Trang will have intense sunlight from May to August.
If you travel to Nha Trang during the hot season, make sure to prepare items such as sunscreen, a wide-brimmed hat, light, breathable clothing to absorb sweat, and so on, to comfortably take part in outdoor activities.
The rainy season in Nha Trang usually starts from September to December. During this time, there will be light rains, the weather becomes cool, but the temperature is not too low.

### How to get to Nha Trang from India
From India to Hanoi/ Ho Chi Minh City
There are no direct flights from India to Nha Trang. So, visitors have to take flights to major cities in Vietnam such as Ho Chi Minh City or Hanoi.
It is the best to travel to Ho Chi Minh City because it is quite close to Nha Trang. The international airport in Ho Chi Minh is Tan Son Nhat and the one in Hanoi is Noi Bai. The flight ticket ranges from 280 USD to 450 USD. Be careful to check out flight schedule before departure.
From Hanoi/ Ho Chi Minh City to Nha Trang
After taking flights to major cities in Vietnam, travellers can take a domestic flight to Cam Ranh Airport, which is the nearest airport to Nha Trang.
Another option is to take a bus from Ho Chi Minh City to Nha Trang. Time travel by bus can be up to 9 hours. Therefore, make sure to have good health if you want to travel this way.
Nha Trang is not too big, with the main road running along the beach. Therefore, it’s recommended that visitors rent a motorbike to explore the city, especially if they want to enjoy the local cuisine. Motorbikes can be rented for around 100,000 – 150,000 VND. Usually, hotels will provide this service for their guests.
Tran Phu is the main road running along the beach, where there are many hotels in Nha Trang for tourists to choose from. Some suggested hotels include Muong Thanh, Novotel, InterContinental Nha Trang, Sheraton Nha Trang, Lodge, etc. With the advantage of being close to the beach and the city center, hotels on this road cost from 900,000 dong per night, while higher-end options can range from 2 to 3 million dong per night.
To save costs, tourists can look for hotels or homestays beyond Tran Phu, specifically on Nguyen Thi Minh Khai, Hung Vuong, or Nguyen Thien Thuat streets.
For tourists who love resort-style vacations, with privacy and away from the city center, Amiana Resort on Pham Van Dong Street, Vinpearl Resort on Hon Tre Island, and MerPerle Resort are suitable choices.

### Top things to do in Nha Trang
There are plenty of things to do in Nha Trang for visitors of all interests, from soaking up the sun on the beach to exploring cultural landmarks and enjoying the city’s nightlife:
- Go beach-hunting: Nha Trang Beach is a popular spot for sunbathing, but Long Beach and nearby beaches like Dốc Lết and Đại Lãnh offer quieter alternatives.
- Try a mud bath: Nha Trang is known for its mud baths and hot springs. Spa facilities in the hills north of the city offer this unique wellness experience.
- Enjoy water activities: Take an island excursion to explore the beautiful shores and small islands near Nha Trang, including Monkey Island, Hòn Mun Island, and Yến Island. Here, enjoy yourself in top playful activities such as swimming in the cool blue water, snorkeling, skydiving…
- Visit pagodas and palaces: Long Sơn Pagoda and the Po Nagar Cham Towers are must-visit sites in Nha Trang. The Bảo Đại Villa to the south also offers insight into the region’s history.
- Join the party: Nha Trang’s nightlife scene is lively and varied, with beachfront parties at Sailing Club and Skylight, and other venues around town that keep the party going late into the night.
Besides seafood, there are many other local specialties and delicious dishes in Nha Trang, such as squid pancakes, grilled pork sausage, fish cake noodle soup, mini rice pancakes, wet rice paper… Below are some food addresses and dining spots in Nha Trang:
- Lac Canh’s Grilled Beef – 44 Nguyen Binh Khiem
- Thanh Da Goat Restaurant – 66 Nguyen Thi Minh Khai
- Anh Sang Oxtail Hotpot – 126 Nguyen Khuyen
- Hoa Grilled Beef and Beef Wrapped in Betel Leaf – 9 Co Bac (only open in the morning)
- Ba Le Bread – 2/4 Street, Number 2
- Di Loan Rice Paper Rolls – 16A Hong Linh
- Hiep Grilled Pork Sausage – 96 Luong Dinh Cua
- Small Pan Squid and Shrimp Pancake – 3 Tran Kim Hung
- Big Pan Pancake – Dam Market
- Damm Fish Cake Noodle Soup – 24 Nguyen Cong Tru
- Le Sea Snails – 76 Vo Tru
- Van Don Alley Snails – 89/5 Van Don Street
- Son Cang Seafood Restaurant – 142 Pham Van Dong

### Vietnam Itineraries with Nha Trang
The average temperature in Nha Trang is around 26-27°C throughout the year. The best time to visit Nha Trang is from January to August. This is the dry season with sunny weather and calm seas. The rainy season in Nha Trang usually starts in October and lasts until the end of the year. So this is not the right time for traveling.
Yes, Indian citizens will need a visa to travel to Vietnam, including Nha Trang. Visitors can apply for a visa online or through the Vietnamese embassy in India.
Nha Trang has many popular tourist attractions. If you are beach lovers, you can drop by beautiful beaches such as Dai Lanh Beach, Long Beach, Doc Let Beach,… Also, there are islands for snorkeling and scuba diving like Hon Mun Island, Hon Tam Island, Hon Mot Island,… If you want to find out more about Vietnamese history and culture, you can save these places to visit: Po Nagar Temple, Long Son Pagoda, Tu Van Pagoda,…
Nha Trang’s transportation system is well-developed. You can choose public vehicles like taxis, and buses. If you want to explore this coastal city freely, you can hire a motorbike at an affordable price ranging from 4 USD to 8 USD. Visitors would find it easy to get around Nha Trang.
Yes, the weather in Nha Trang is generally suitable for Indian tourists. Nha Trang has a tropical climate with two distinct seasons: the rainy season from September to December and the dry season from January to August. The average temperature in Nha Trang is around 26-27°C throughout the year, which is similar to the weather in many parts of India. However, Indian tourists should be aware of the high humidity levels in Nha Trang, especially during the rainy season, and take necessary precautions to stay hydrated and cool.

## Ninh Binh: All Your Need To Know Before You Go From India
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_ninh-binh/
Pricing seen on page: ₹40,052 – ₹79,325 per person
Captivating region often referred to as ‘Ha Long Bay on Land’
A great addition to your Vietnam tour package is Ninh Binh. Located approximately 100 kilometers from Hanoi, Ninh Binh is a perfect option for travelers who may not have much time but want to explore magnificent natural landscapes.
This captivating region is often considered as ‘Ha Long Bay on Land’ thanks to its enchanting riverine landscape, where towering limestone mountains loom over verdant paddies. To truly appreciate this UNESCO-protected site, taking a paddleboat tour along the glistening rivers and scaling the legendary peaks is highly recommended.
The best time to visit Ninh Binh is during the months of March to May and September to November, with harvest season in October providing the finest views. However, if visiting during the hotter months of July and August, be prepared for unpredictable downpours and stormy weather.

### How to get to Ninh Binh from India
Regular buses depart from Hanoi to Ninh Binh daily. Luxury vans are also available, providing transportation to Tam Coc, where a variety of charming accommodations can be found. Private cars offer the quickest option for travel. For those with more time, trains also depart from Hanoi to Ninh Binh daily.
From Hanoi, travelers can book a Limousine to Ninh Binh. The ticket price ranges from 100,000 – 150,000 VND, and the travel time is about 2 hours.
If departing from Ho Chi Minh City, tourists can fly to Hanoi and take a coach to Ninh Binh with the above options. Vietnamese airlines such as Vietnam Airlines, Vietjet Air, and Jetstar all operate flights from Ho Chi Minh City to Hanoi. Currently, ticket prices range from 900,000 – 1.3 million VND/ one-way.
To get around Ninh Binh, motorcycle tours or taxis are recommended, while cycling is a great option for those staying in Tam Coc.
Travelers visiting Ninh Binh, whether as couples or groups, can choose to stay at homestays located near popular tourist spots with serene and green surroundings, friendly service, and a peaceful atmosphere. Some popular homestays with convenient locations are Ninh Binh Friendly Homestay, Hang Múa Ecolodge, Diep’s House, Ninh Binh Palm Homestay, ChezBeo Homestay, with prices ranging from around 200,000 VND per person per night.
For those seeking more luxurious amenities such as swimming pools, spas, gyms, and bars, there are several 4-5 star resorts to choose from, including Emeralda Resort Ninh Binh, Tam Coc Garden Resort, Ninh Binh Hidden Charm Hotel And Resort, An’s Eco Garden Resort, Aravinda Resort Ninh Binh. Room rates range from 1.7 to 6 million VND per night.

### Top things to do in Ninh Binh
- See the view from Hang Mua: Get a bird’s-eye view of Ninh Binh by climbing the 500 steps carved into the steep face of the Hang Mua mountain. The lookout point offers a surreal vista of the winding river, paddies, and limestone outcrops.
- Take a sampan tour: Guided by a boat person who rows with their feet, not hands, the sampan tours at Tam Coc and Trang An provide an expertly guided journey along gentle waterways, towering karsts, and into dripping grottoes.
- Visit the ancient capital: Hoa Lu, the capital of Vietnam during the 10th and 11th centuries, is a small ancient city naturally protected from invasions by karsts and rivers. It’s an ideal place for history enthusiasts to visit.
- Go in search of wildlife: At the Van Long Nature Reserve, a rowboat ride is a must-do experience. Be on the lookout for exotic wildlife, such as the black-faced spoonbill and Delacour’s Langur, one of the world’s most endangered primates.
- Pay a visit to the Bai Dinh Temple: This complex of Buddhist temples, covering an area of 500 hectares, is the largest in the country. Ancient temples can be found in caves only reachable after climbing 300 steps, while the new temple area features a mix of old and new
When in Ninh Binh, don’t miss out on local specialties such as crispy rice, raw goat meat with lime, stir-fried goat meat, grilled goat meat, crab stir-fried with betel leaf, and Vietnamese herring salad.
Goat meat is a favorite dish among many visitors to Ninh Binh. The goats are raised on the rocky mountains, resulting in firm and chewy meat. To find reputable restaurants with quality mountain goats, diners should head to mountainous areas such as Hoa Lu, around Trang An Ecotourism Area, and Tam Coc. Each serving of goat meat costs around 100,000 to 250,000 VND, depending on the preparation method.
Here are some recommended restaurants in Ninh Binh:
- Chinh Thu goat meat restaurant (Khe Thuong, Hoa Lu, Ninh Binh)
- Duc De restaurant (446 Nguyen Hue, Ninh Binh City)
- Dung Pho Nui restaurant (Ninh Xuan commune, Hoa Lu, Ninh Binh)
- Mrs. Phan’s eel vermicelli soup (999 Tran Hung Dao, Ninh Binh city)
- To Nhu’s meatball vermicelli soup (National Highway 10, Kim Son, Ninh Binh)
- Thanh Hang sticky rice ice cream (Luong Van Tuy Street, Ninh Binh City)

You can take a flight from India to Hanoi or Ho Chi Minh City, and then take a bus or train to Ninh Binh. The distance between Hanoi/Ho Chi Minh City and Ninh Binh is about 90km and 1100km respectively.
The best time to visit Ninh Binh is during the dry season, which is from November to April. During this time, the weather is pleasant, and you can enjoy outdoor activities and sightseeing.
There are several holy places to visit in Ninh Binh, such as Bai Dinh Pagoda, Hoa Lu Ancient Capital, and Phat Diem Cathedral. These places have significant cultural and historical value and offer beautiful architectural designs and stunning scenery.
You should pack comfortable clothing and footwear suitable for outdoor activities, such as hiking or biking. Don’t forget to bring sunscreen, insect repellent, and a hat to protect yourself from the sun. It’s also recommended to bring a raincoat or umbrella during the rainy season.
While Ninh Binh is not as well-known for its vegan/vegetarian cuisine as Hanoi, there are still some options available. You can try Chay Ninh Binh restaurant, Hoa Lu vegan restaurant, Aroma Indian restaurants… which serve Vietnamese vegetarian food, or ask your hotel for recommendations.
It’s recommended to spend at least 2-3 days in Ninh Binh to explore the main attractions, such as Tam Coc, Trang An, and Van Long Nature Reserve. However, you can also extend your stay to enjoy the peaceful countryside and immerse yourself in the local culture.
Some popular souvenirs to buy in Ninh Binh include embroidered items, bamboo products, and handmade pottery. You can find these items at local markets or souvenir shops.

## Phu Quoc, Vietnam: Ultimate Travel Guide For Indian Tourists
URL: https://vietnamtour.in/destinations_phu-quoc/
Pricing seen on page: ₹39,845 – ₹79,325 per person
Ultimate Travel Guide for Indian Tourists in 2026
Phu Quoc, the “Pearl Island,” is a perfect tropical destination for Indian tourists looking for a beautiful tropical getaway during their trip to Vietnam. This Vietnamese treasure not only has stunning white sand beaches and beautiful waters, but it also has a remarkable dedication to protecting its environment and cultural legacy.
With so much to offer, Phu Quoc truly lives up to its reputation as a “heaven on Earth”. As a trustworthy companion, Vietnamtour.in is here to help you make the most of your journey.

### Best Time to Visit Phu Quoc
The best time to visit Phu Quoc is during the dry season, from November to April, with many sunny days and calm waters. The temperature fluctuates from 25 to 35°C, and the low rainfall provides perfect conditions for water sports and adventurous activities. March and April are the hottest months of the year in Phu Quoc, with an average temperature above 32°C.
May to October is the wet season and possibly stormy in Phu Quoc. During this time, the region has high rainfall, with an average temperature hovering around 28°C and humidity ranging from 85 to 90%. Many days experience cloudy and light sunshine, but afternoons and evenings have heavy rains.

### How to Travel from India to Phu Quoc

**Stage 1: From India to Vietnam**
Booking an international flight from one of India’s major cities, like New Delhi, Mumbai, Bangalore, Chennai, or Kolkata, is the first step. Tan Son Nhat International Airport in Ho Chi Minh City or Noi Bai International Airport in Hanoi are most likely to be your destinations within Vietnam.

**Stage 2: From Hanoi or Ho Chi Minh City to Phu Quoc Island**
Taking a domestic flight is the quickest and most convenient way to get to Phu Quoc once in Vietnam. Both Ho Chi Minh City and Hanoi offer multiple daily flights to Phu Quoc International Airport. The flight time from Ho Chi Minh City is approximately 1 hour, while from Hanoi, it takes around 2 hours and 10 minutes.
By private car and high-speed ferries
You can take a bus or hire a private car to reach the coastal cities of Rach Gia or Ha Tien. The road journey takes around 6 to 8 hours, depending on traffic and weather conditions.
From Rach Gia or Ha Tien, high-speed ferries run daily services to Phu Quoc Island. The ferry ride takes about 2 to 3 hours. Departures usually start in the early morning and continue until late afternoon.
Exploring Phu Quoc Island is easy and fun. Visitors have various options to get around the island, such as renting a motorbike for around 120,000-200,000 VND per day, taking a taxi, renting a bicycle for around 30,000-100,000 VND per day, or renting a private car with a driver for around 1,000,000 VND per day. However, public transportation is not as frequent, so it’s recommended to plan your transportation in advance to ensure a smooth and enjoyable trip.
Don’t want to plan everything yourself? Check out Vietnamtour.in’s handpicked Phu Quoc tour packages covering these top attractions, designed especially for Indian tourists.
Often referred to as the “Sleepless City,” Grand World Phu Quoc is a vibrant entertainment and shopping complex that operates 24/7. Visitors can enjoy cultural shows, Venice-style canal boat rides, colorful night markets, and a wide range of dining and shopping options.
VinWonders is the largest theme park in Vietnam, offering a mix of thrilling rides, water slides, and interactive zones inspired by fairy tales and world civilizations. It’s an ideal day out for families with children, with areas like Adventure World, Water World, and European Avenue providing hours of fun.
Covering over half of the island, Phu Quoc National Park is a haven for nature lovers. The park offers hiking trails through dense tropical forests, bird-watching opportunities, and access to Mount Chua.
Vietnam’s first and only open zoo, Vinpearl Safari is home to over 3,000 animals from 150 species worldwide. The park features an open-range safari, where visitors can experience a close-up view of wildlife from a safari bus, and a zoo area with educational shows and feeding sessions.
Located on the western coast, Long Beach offers over 20 kilometers of golden sand and calm waters. It’s perfect for sunbathing, swimming, and watching sunset views. Several luxury resorts, beachfront bars, and restaurants line this stretch.
Ganh Dau is a quiet fishing village offering panoramic views of the Gulf of Thailand and the Cambodian coastline on clear days. Visitors can explore small local seafood restaurants, stroll along the rocky shorelines, and enjoy a more laid-back part of Phu Quoc.
Starfish Beach (Rach Vem)
Famous for its shallow waters dotted with red starfish, Starfish Beach offers unique photo opportunities and a peaceful setting. While the beach itself is rustic, visitors love the wooden seafood shacks serving freshly caught fish, crabs, and other local specialties.
Phu Quoc’s pepper is famous across Vietnam, and a visit to one of the island’s pepper farms in the north gives insight into how this spice is grown and harvested. Guests can walk among the pepper vines, learn about the drying process, and purchase fresh pepper products as souvenirs.
Arguably the most beautiful beach on the island, Star Beach is known for its powdery white sand and turquoise water. It’s a favorite spot for swimming, snorkeling, and beach photography.
Phu Quoc Cable Car & Hon Thom Island
The world’s longest oversea cable car connects An Thoi Town to Hon Thom Island, offering breathtaking aerial views of the An Thoi Archipelago. On Hon Thom Island, visitors can relax on pristine beaches, try water sports, or visit the Sun World Hon Thom Nature Park for fun activities.
This group of small islands to the south of Phu Quoc is a paradise for snorkeling and island hopping. Popular spots include Hon May Rut, Hon Gam Ghi, and Hon Mong Tay, where visitors can explore coral reefs, swim in clear waters, and enjoy boat tours with seafood lunches.
Coconut Prison (Phu Quoc Prison Museum)
A sobering historical site, Coconut Prison tells the story of Vietnamese prisoners during the wars. The museum features reconstructed prison cells, torture instruments, and exhibitions about the harsh living conditions.
Located on the east coast but often visited during South Island tours, Ham Ninh offers a glimpse of traditional fishing life. The village is famous for fresh seafood, especially crabs. Walking along the long wooden pier at sunrise or sunset is a memorable experience.
A popular picnic spot, Suoi Tranh is a small waterfall surrounded by lush greenery and rock formations. After a short forest walk, visitors can reach the falls and enjoy the cool water. It’s best visited during the rainy season when the water flow is stronger.
Phu Quoc Night Market (Duong Dong Market)
Located in the heart of Duong Dong Town, the Phu Quoc Night Market is a bustling area filled with food stalls, souvenir shops, and fresh seafood vendors. It’s the perfect place to try local dishes like grilled scallops, seafood hotpot, and Phu Quoc fish sauce-based recipes.
Dinh Cau Temple (Cau Temple)
Perched on a rocky outcrop overlooking the sea, Dinh Cau Temple is both a spiritual site and a scenic viewpoint. Fishermen come here to pray for safe journeys, and tourists often visit at sunset to capture beautiful coastal photos.
Phu Quoc is known as the “Pearl Island” for a reason. Visiting a pearl farm allows travelers to learn about the pearl cultivation process, from oyster farming to harvesting and polishing. Many farms also have showrooms where visitors can buy authentic Phu Quoc pearls.
Made from the local rose myrtle fruit (Sim), Sim wine is a unique Phu Quoc specialty. A visit to one of the island’s Sim wine factories offers a short tour of the production process and the chance to sample different varieties. The wine is sweet, fragrant, and often bought as a souvenir.

### Top Things to Do in Phu Quoc
Adventures in Snorkeling and Island-Hopping
Discover the smaller islets surrounding Phu Quoc for sailing, swimming, and snorkeling. Snorkelers will love Turtle Island (Hon Doi Moi) and Fingernail Island (Hon Mong Tay), which are well-known for their pristine waters and colorful coral reefs. Adventurers can enjoy diving and water sports at Dragon Rock (Ghenh Da Ban) and Hon Thom (Pineapple Island), which are further south.
Wildlife Encounters at Vinpearl Safari
Visit Vinpearl Safari Phu Quoc, Vietnam’s first and only open zoo built to international safari standards. The park is home to animals from Africa, Europe, India, Australia, and more. Take a safari bus ride to see lions, rhinos, giraffes, zebras, and tigers roaming in semi-wild habitats. Families with children will especially enjoy the interactive feeding and photo zones.
Shopping and Street Food at Phu Quoc Night Markets
Spend your evening at the Phu Quoc Night Market in Duong Dong Town (mainly along Vo Thi Sau Street near Dinh Cau Temple). The market opens daily from around 6:00 pm to 10:30 pm, offering fresh seafood, tropical fruits, street snacks, souvenirs, and pearl jewelry. Bargaining is common and part of the fun.
Sunset Cocktails at Beach Bars
After a day of sightseeing, unwind with a sunset cocktail at one of Phu Quoc’s beach bars. Popular choices include Rory’s Beach Bar, Sunset Sanato Beach Club, OCSEN Beach Bar & Club, and Rock Sunset Island Bar. These venues offer a laid-back atmosphere with music, drinks, and stunning sea views.
Relax on Phu Quoc’s Best Beaches
No trip to Phu Quoc is complete without time on its beautiful, uncrowded beaches. Top choices include Long Beach (Bai Truong), Ong Lang Beach, Khem Beach, and Sao Beach (Bai Sao). For the more adventurous, explore hidden beaches that are not marked on tourist maps and enjoy your own private slice of paradise.
Ride the World’s Longest Cable Car
Experience the record-breaking Phu Quoc, Hon Thom Cable Car, stretching nearly 8 kilometers across the sea. The 15-minute ride offers spectacular aerial views of the Gulf of Thailand. Once at Hon Thom Island, visitors can enjoy beach activities, water parks, and local dining. The cable car operates daily from 7:30 am to 5:30 pm, with a short lunch break around midday.
Phu Quoc Island is not only known for its beaches and nature but also for its rich and diverse local cuisine, with seafood being the culinary highlight. Our local guides at Vietnamtour.in can recommend the best seafood restaurants and help arrange Indian-friendly menus upon request

**Enjoy Fresh Seafood at Local Fishing Villages**
Phu Quoc offers an abundance of fresh seafood. Must-try dishes include clams, abalone, mudskipper fish, sea cucumbers, and sea snails. For a truly local experience, head to fishing villages. For those preferring a more comfortable dining environment, the restaurants below are favorites among both locals and tourists. Expect to spend around 400,000 to 600,000 VND per person, depending on your order.
- Ra Khoi Seafood Restaurant (30/4 Street, Duong Dong Town)
- Xin Chao Seafood Restaurant (66 Tran Hung Dao Street)
- Rach Vem and Cua Can Fishing Villages
A unique local specialty that every visitor should try is Bun Quay. This dish features fresh rice noodles, squid, fish cake, and shrimp cake, prepared on-site. The highlight is the self-mixed dipping sauce, where diners combine lime, chili, soup powder, sugar, and other seasonings to their taste. Each bowl costs approximately 35,000 VND, making it a budget-friendly and flavorful meal option.
- Bun Quay Kien Xay (Near Dinh Cau Night Market)

**Explore Other Local Eateries**
For variety beyond seafood, Phu Quoc has several well-loved local restaurants.
- Ho Ky Goat Restaurant (30/4 Street) is popular for its goat hotpot and grilled goat dishes.
- Bo To Beef Restaurant (100 Tran Hung Dao Street) is known for grilled beef and beef hotpot
- Nha Xua Rice Pot Restaurant (68 Ly Thuong Kiet Street) offers home-style Vietnamese clay pot rice dishes.

### Things to Buy in Phu Quoc
Some famous specialties of Phu Quoc that you can buy as gifts are pearls, fish sauce, pepper, and sim wine:
- Phu Quoc pearls: relatively high price, depending on their quality, color, and size. Some reputable pearl shops to consider are Ngoc Hien and Quoc An.
- Phu Quoc fish sauce: famous for its delicious and pure flavor. Some reputable fish sauce manufacturers to consider are Phung Hung, Khai Hoan, Thinh Phat, and Hung Thinh.
- Phu Quoc pepper: has a spicy and strong aroma, with firm grains. You can buy pepper grains right at the Khu Tuong pepper garden or the Suoi Da pepper garden, which are well-known addresses.
- Sim wine: in Phu Quoc is traditionally fermented from rose myrtle and white sand sugar. This type of wine is good for digestion and relieving fatigue. Familiar places to buy sim wine are Sim Son and Bay Giao sim wine.
- Fresh seafood: Sea cucumbers, squid, and various types of dried fish. The night market in Duong Dong has many stores selling these products, with prices depending on the season and size.
